<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that critical Bernoulli bond percolation has no infinite cluster on any infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with critical probability less than one. This resolves the bond form of the criticality conjecture of Benjamini and Schramm.

- **claim-main-nonpercolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive undirected graph. If $p_c(G)<1$, then $$\mathbb P_{p_c(G)}(\text{there is an infinite open cluster})=0.$$ Equivalently, $\mathbb P_{p_c(G)}(\|C_x\|=\infty)=0$ for every vertex $x$.
  - Explanation: At the critical parameter an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with critical probability below one has no infinite open cluster, so the Benjamini–Schramm conjecture holds for the bond model.
- **claim-orbit-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-orbit-growth)): There is an integer $C_0$ such that every vertex orbit meets every ball of radius $C_0$. Moreover, for nonnegative integer radii, $$\label{eq:ball-comparisons} V_+(r)\le V_-(r+C_0),\qquad V_+(r+s)\le V_+(r)V_+(s).$$ The limit $\gamma=\lim_{r\to\infty}V_+(r)^{1/r}$ exists. If $\gamma>1$, every vertex ball has exponential growth; if $\gamma=1$, then $\log V_+(r)=o(r)$.
  - Explanation: Every vertex orbit is uniformly dense, so extremal ball volumes compare up to a bounded shift of the radius and the exponential growth rate exists.
- **claim-exponential-growth-external** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-exponential)): If a connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph satisfies $\liminf_{r\to\infty}\|B(x,r)\|^{1/r}>1$, then it has almost surely no infinite Bernoulli bond cluster at its critical probability.
  - Explanation: Imported external theorem: exponential growth already rules out an infinite critical cluster, which leaves only subexponential growth to treat.
- **claim-two-cluster-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-two-cluster)): Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph of subexponential growth. For every $p_*>0$ there is $C<\infty$ such that $$\mathbb P_p(H_s(e))\le C s^{-1/2} \qquad(e\in E(G),\ s\ge1,\ p_*\le p<1).$$ The constant may depend on $G$ and $p_*$.
  - Explanation: On subexponential quasi-transitive graphs two distinct large clusters across a fixed edge have probability decaying like an inverse square root of their size, uniformly in the edge.
- **claim-avoidance-correlation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-avoidance)): Let $G$ be a finite undirected graph with independent edge probabilities strictly between zero and one. Let $A,B$ be disjoint nonempty vertex sets. Conditional on $A\not\leftrightarrow B$, each of the two cluster objects $C(A)$ and $C(B)$ is associated. If $f$ and $g$ are increasing functions of the respective objects, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}\bigl(f(C(A)),g(C(B))\mid A\not\leftrightarrow B\bigr)\le0.$$
  - Explanation: Conditioning two root sets to avoid each other keeps each cluster object associated and makes increasing functionals of the two objects nonpositively correlated.
- **claim-dfs-pivotal-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-dfs)): For the preceding search, let $$W=\sum_{e\text{ queried actual open pivot}}q d_e.$$ Then $\mathbb E_\mu W\le1$ and $\mathbb E_\mu W^2\le3$.
  - Explanation: The total weight of queried actual open pivots in the lazy depth-first search has controlled first and second moments.
- **claim-transcript-entropy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy)): Let $\mu$ be the Bernoulli product law of parameter $0<p<1$ on a finite set of bits, and let $Q$ be any probability law on those bits. Consider finitely many terminating deterministic adaptive query algorithms, each reading any bit at most once. Let $Q_i,\mu_i$ be their transcript laws. Suppose that, on the support of $Q$, every bit is read by at most $a$ algorithms, where $a\ge1$ is an integer. Then $$\sum_iD(Q_i\Vert\mu_i) \le\frac{a^2}{2p(1-p)}D(Q\Vert\mu).$$
  - Explanation: Relative entropies of several adaptive transcripts add up to at most a multiple of the relative entropy of the common configuration, with the multiple set by the overlap bound.
- **claim-path-interpolation** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-interpolation)): Let $\mu$ be Bernoulli bond percolation with parameter $p\in(0,1)$ on a finite undirected graph, and put $q=1-p$. Let $x_0,x_1,\ldots,x_d$ be a simple path, with $d\ge2$. Suppose that $$\mu(x_j\leftrightarrow x_k)\ge c>0 \qquad(0\le j<k\le d,\ k-j<d).$$ For an integer $s\ge1$, suppose that the clusters of $x_0,x_d$ are distinct and both have at least $s$ vertices with probability $\delta>0$. There is a path edge $e$ such that $$-\log\mu(H_s(e)) \le C\bigl(\log(1/\delta)+\lceil\log_2d\rceil+1\bigr).$$ Here $H_s(e)$ is the event that the endpoint clusters of $e$ are distinct and each has at least $s$ vertices. The constant $C$ depends only on $c$ and positive lower bounds for $p$ and $q$.
  - Explanation: Along a path whose endpoints carry two distinct large clusters with probability delta, some path edge has an adjacent-large-clusters probability bounded below in terms of delta and the logarithm of the path length.
- **claim-superpolynomial-nonpercolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-superpoly)): Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with $p_c<1$. Suppose that $$\log V_+(r)=o(r), \qquad \frac{\log V_-(r)}{\log r}\longrightarrow\infty.$$ Then critical Bernoulli bond percolation on $G$ has almost surely no infinite cluster.
  - Explanation: In the subexponential branch with growth faster than every power, critical percolation has no infinite cluster almost surely.
- **claim-joint-gluing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-joint-gluing)): Let $G$ have countably many vertices and edges, with independent edge-open probabilities in $[0,1]$. For a vertex $o$, a nonempty set $A$ of vertices, and any set $T$ of vertices, $$\label{eq:joint-gluing} \mathbb P(o\leftrightarrow A,\ o\leftrightarrow T) \ge \mathbb P(o\leftrightarrow A) \inf_{a\in A}\mathbb P(a\leftrightarrow T).$$ For finite $A$, the infimum is a minimum.
  - Explanation: The probability of connecting a vertex both to a relay set and to a target set is at least the probability of reaching the relay set times the least target probability over relays.
- **claim-deleted-residual** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-deleted-residual)): Fix a finite product graph $G$, a vertex $s$, a vertex set $S$ not containing $s$, and an increasing function $F$ on sets that contain $s$ and avoid $S$. For $w\supseteq S$ with $s\notin w$, define $$c(w)=\mathbb E^{G\setminus w}F(C(s)).$$ For $U\subseteq V(G)\setminus(S\cup\{s\})$, sample $W_U=C^{G\setminus U}(S)$ and define $$R_F(U)=\mathbb E\left[ \mathbf1_{\{s\notin W_U\}} \bigl(c(W_U)-c(W_U\cup U)\bigr)\right],$$ where the integrand is zero when $s\in W_U$. Then $R_F$ is nonnegative and increasing under inclusion of $U$.
  - Explanation: The deleted-set residual is nonnegative and grows with the deleted set; it supplies the nonnegative terms in the column decomposition.
- **claim-cluster-column-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cluster-column)): For every nonnegative increasing $F$ on $\mathcal D_k$, $$\label{eq:cluster-column} v_k^F-(\mathbb E_{\mu_k}F)h_k \in\left\{\sum_{l>k}a_lh_l:a_l\ge0\right\}.$$ In particular $H^{-1}v_k^F$ has nonnegative coordinates.
  - Explanation: The centered covariance vector of an increasing functional lies in the cone of later columns, so the inverse of the column matrix gives nonnegative coordinates.
- **claim-gluing-failure** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-gluing-failure)): In the setting of Theorem <a href="#thm:joint-gluing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:joint-gluing">10</a>, let $0\le\lambda\le1$. If $\mathbb P(a\leftrightarrow T)\ge1-\lambda$ for every $a\in A$, then $$\mathbb P(o\leftrightarrow A,\ o\not\leftrightarrow T) \le\lambda\,\mathbb P(o\leftrightarrow A)\le\lambda.$$ The same conclusion holds for $A=\varnothing$.
  - Explanation: If every relay reaches the target with high probability, the chance of reaching a relay but then failing to reach the target is small.
- **claim-structured-nonpercolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-structured)): Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite graph of subexponential growth. Suppose a group $\Lambda$ acts by automorphisms on $G$ with finitely many vertex orbits and admits an epimorphism $\varrho:\Lambda\to N$ onto a finitely generated nilpotent group, such that $\ker\varrho$ has finite vertex orbits and $\varrho(\Lambda_v)$ is finite for every vertex $v$. Here $\Lambda_v=\{g\in\Lambda:gv=v\}$ is the vertex stabilizer. If $p_c(G)<1$, then $$\mathbb P_{p_c(G)}(\|C_v\|=\infty)=0\qquad(v\in G).$$ Consequently, critical percolation has almost surely no infinite cluster.
  - Explanation: A subexponential graph with a group action that has a nilpotent quotient and finite stabilizer images has no infinite critical cluster.
- **claim-nilpotent-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-nilpotent-model)): Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph. Suppose that for some $d<\infty$ there are radii $R_j\to\infty$ such that $V_-(R_j)\le R_j^d$. Then there are a finite-index subgroup $\Lambda\le\operatorname{Aut}(G)$, a finitely generated nilpotent group $N$, and an epimorphism $$\varrho:\Lambda\longrightarrow N$$ with the following properties:* 1. *$\Lambda$ has finitely many vertex orbits;* 2. *$\ker\varrho$ has finite vertex orbits;* 3. *$\varrho(\Lambda_v)$ is finite for every vertex $v$, where $\Lambda_v=\{g\in\Lambda:gv=v\}$.
  - Explanation: A polynomial volume bound along an unbounded sequence of radii yields a finite-index subgroup of the automorphism group with a nilpotent quotient having the action properties needed by the structured theorem.
- **claim-cyclic-series-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rank)): Every finitely generated nilpotent group $N$ has a cyclic series. If such a series has $h$ infinite factors, then every subgroup $J\le N$ is finitely generated and inherits a cyclic series with at most $h$ infinite factors. If $[N:J]=\infty$, the inherited count is strictly less than $h$. Moreover, if the finitely generated abelianization $N/[N,N]$ has rank at most one, then $[N,N]$ is finite. In this case $N$ is either finite or admits an epimorphism onto $\mathbb Z$ with finite kernel.
  - Explanation: Finitely generated nilpotent groups have cyclic series; subgroups inherit them with no more infinite factors, strictly fewer for infinite index, which drives the induction.
- **claim-bounded-projection-exclusion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-unbounded-projection)): Let $(G,\Lambda,\varrho,N)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:structured" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:structured">14</a>, with $p_c(G)<1$. Fix a cyclic series for $N$ with $h$ infinite factors and an epimorphism $\phi:N\to\mathbb Z^2$. Let $\pi$ be given by <a href="#eq:equivariant-projection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:equivariant-projection">[eq:equivariant-projection]</a>. Assume Theorem <a href="#thm:structured" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:structured">14</a> for every such action whose nilpotent quotient has a cyclic series with fewer than $h$ infinite factors. Then critical percolation on $G$ almost surely has no infinite open ray whose $\pi$-projection is bounded.
  - Explanation: Assuming the structured theorem for smaller counts of infinite factors, critical percolation has no infinite open ray with bounded projection.
- **claim-approximate-moves** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-moves)): Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite graph and let a group $\Lambda$ act on it by automorphisms with finitely many vertex orbits. Let $\pi:V(G)\to\mathbb Z^2$ be onto, with edge increments bounded in Euclidean norm by $D_\pi$ and with $\pi(gx)=\pi(x)+a_g$, where $g\mapsto a_g$ is an epimorphism from $\Lambda$ to $\mathbb Z^2$. Suppose $0<p_c<1$ and, at $p_c$, there is almost surely a unique infinite cluster and no infinite open ray with bounded $\pi$-projection. For every $0<e,\eta<1/10$, there is an integer $m$ such that for every requested $\rho_0>0$ one can choose an invertible linear map $\Phi:\mathbb R^2\to\mathbb R^2$ and a parameter $p'\in[p_c/2,p_c)$ with the following properties. The coordinates $(t,z)=\Phi\pi$ have edge increments at most $\rho_0$ in supremum norm. For $y\in V(G)$ and $s\in\{-1,1\}$, let $E_s(y)$ be the event that an open path starts in $B(y,m)$, stays within supremum coordinate distance $100$ of $y$, and ends within supremum distance $e/4$ of $(t(y)+1,z(y)+s)$. Then $$\mathbb P_{p'}(E_s(y))\ge1-\eta \qquad(y\in V(G),\ s\in\{-1,1\}).$$ The radius $m$ is chosen before $\rho_0$.
  - Explanation: Under uniqueness and no bounded-projection rays at the critical parameter, two rescaled approximate coordinate moves persist with high probability at a slightly smaller parameter, with seed radius fixed before the edge increments are made small.
- **claim-uniqueness-subexponential** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-uniqueness)): Let $G$ be infinite, connected, locally finite and quasi-transitive, with $\log V_+(r)=o(r)$. If $p\in(0,1)$ and Bernoulli bond percolation at $p$ has an infinite cluster with positive probability, then it has exactly one infinite cluster almost surely.
  - Explanation: Under subexponential growth, whenever an infinite cluster exists with positive probability it is almost surely unique.
- **claim-corridor-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-corridors)): Distinct $Q_v$ are disjoint. Two distinct corridors with no common endpoint have disjoint vertex sets; corridors sharing an endpoint $v$ intersect only in $Q_v$. A corridor misses every nonendpoint $Q_w$. Consequently, common edges of incident corridors belong to $\mathcal Q_v$ at their common endpoint.
  - Explanation: Endpoint regions are disjoint and corridors meet only inside shared endpoint regions, so outgoing corridors stay fresh when an incoming one is revealed.
- **claim-fresh-collar-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-transfer)): Let $G$ and the parameters $m,k,R,K,\eta_0,\lambda,\kappa,p'$ satisfy the choices in Subsection <a href="#subsec:corridor-scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:corridor-scales">9.2</a>. Consider a countable product graph whose edges are original edges of $G$, together with a synthetic source $*$ joined by deterministic open edges to finitely many vertices. Original edges have arbitrary independent probabilities except where specified below. Let $T_*$ be a fixed vertex set in this product graph. Let $B_*$ be a vertex set whose distance-$R$ collar is contained in a subgraph $D_*$ having all its induced original edges present and iid with probability $p'$. Suppose each vertex $y$ in the collar satisfies $$\mathbb P_{p'}(B(y,m)\leftrightarrow T_*\text{ by paths in }D_*) \ge1-\eta_0.$$ Suppose the wired seed vertices are outside the collar, and all other edges of the product graph are original edges of $G$. Then $$\label{eq:fresh-collar-transfer} \mathbb P(*\leftrightarrow B_*,\ *\nleftrightarrow T_*)\le\kappa.$$ In particular this applies to each successive pair in <a href="#eq:relay-regions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:relay-regions">[eq:relay-regions]</a>, whenever the whole corridor is fresh and the wires are outside the input collar.
  - Explanation: Reaching a region through a fresh collar but missing the target has probability at most kappa, using trial seeds, joint gluing and a shell with enough contacts.
- **claim-subcritical-exploration** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-exploration)): Suppose the coordinates and seed radius satisfy Proposition <a href="#prop:moves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:moves">18</a>, the corridor layout satisfies Lemma <a href="#lem:corridors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:corridors">20</a>, and every relay in <a href="#eq:relay-regions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:relay-regions">[eq:relay-regions]</a> satisfies Proposition <a href="#prop:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transfer">21</a>. If $$2(\eta_0+L\kappa)<\delta,\qquad \delta+2(L+1)\kappa/\delta\le\varepsilon, \qquad\sum_{l\ge1}4^l\varepsilon^{l/4}<1,$$ then Bernoulli bond percolation at $p'$ has an infinite cluster with positive probability. In particular, the choices <a href="#eq:corridor-trial-scales" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:corridor-trial-scales">[eq:corridor-trial-scales]</a>–<a href="#eq:corridor-coordinate-step" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:corridor-coordinate-step">[eq:corridor-coordinate-step]</a> and the preceding error parameters have this consequence at $p'<p_c$.
  - Explanation: Adaptive exploration of corridors with stored predictions and a failed-site boundary count gives an infinite cluster with positive probability below the critical parameter, contradicting the assumed critical jump.
- **claim-lattice-corollary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-lattices)): For nearest-neighbor Bernoulli bond percolation on $\mathbb Z^d$, for every $d\ge2$, there is almost surely no infinite cluster at the critical probability. In particular this holds on $\mathbb Z^3$.
  - Explanation: Nearest-neighbor bond percolation on every integer lattice of dimension at least two has no infinite cluster at criticality, because the critical probability is below one and the main theorem applies.

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be Bernoulli bond percolation with parameter $p\\in(0,1)$ on a finite undirected graph, and put $q=1-p$. Let $x_0,x_1,\\ldots,x_d$ be a simple path, with $d\\ge2$. Suppose that $$\\mu(x_j\\leftrightarrow x_k)\\ge c>0\n \\qquad(0\\le j<k\\le d,\\ k-j<d).$$ For an integer $s\\ge1$, suppose that the clusters of $x_0,x_d$ are distinct and both have at least $s$ vertices with probability $\\delta>0$. There is a path edge $e$ such that $$-\\log\\mu(H_s(e))\n \\le C\\bigl(\\log(1/\\delta)+\\lceil\\log_2d\\rceil+1\\bigr).$$ Here $H_s(e)$ is the event that the endpoint clusters of $e$ are distinct and each has at least $s$ vertices. The constant $C$ depends only on $c$ and positive lower bounds for $p$ and $q$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-joint-gluing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ have countably many vertices and edges, with independent edge-open probabilities in $[0,1]$. For a vertex $o$, a nonempty set $A$ of vertices, and any set $T$ of vertices, $$\\label{eq:joint-gluing}\n \\mathbb P(o\\leftrightarrow A,\\ o\\leftrightarrow T)\n \\ge \\mathbb P(o\\leftrightarrow A)\n          \\inf_{a\\in A}\\mathbb P(a\\leftrightarrow T).$$ For finite $A$, the infimum is a minimum.",
        "source_id": "PM-213.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-213.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-deleted-residual",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite product graph $G$, a vertex $s$, a vertex set $S$ not containing $s$, and an increasing function $F$ on sets that contain $s$ and avoid $S$. For $w\\supseteq S$ with $s\\notin w$, define $$c(w)=\\mathbb E^{G\\setminus w}F(C(s)).$$ For $U\\subseteq V(G)\\setminus(S\\cup\\{s\\})$, sample $W_U=C^{G\\setminus U}(S)$ and define $$R_F(U)=\\mathbb E\\left[\n  \\mathbf1_{\\{s\\notin W_U\\}}\n       \\bigl(c(W_U)-c(W_U\\cup U)\\bigr)\\right],$$ where the integrand is zero when $s\\in W_U$. Then $R_F$ is nonnegative and increasing under inclusion of $U$.",
        "source_id": "PM-213.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-213.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-structured",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite graph of subexponential growth. Suppose a group $\\Lambda$ acts by automorphisms on $G$ with finitely many vertex orbits and admits an epimorphism $\\varrho:\\Lambda\\to N$ onto a finitely generated nilpotent group, such that $\\ker\\varrho$ has finite vertex orbits and $\\varrho(\\Lambda_v)$ is finite for every vertex $v$. Here $\\Lambda_v=\\{g\\in\\Lambda:gv=v\\}$ is the vertex stabilizer. If $p_c(G)<1$, then $$\\mathbb P_{p_c(G)}(|C_v|=\\infty)=0\\qquad(v\\in G).$$ Consequently, critical percolation has almost surely no infinite cluster.",
        "source_id": "PM-213.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-structured",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite graph of subexponential growth. Here $\\Lambda_v=\\{g\\in\\Lambda:gv=v\\}$ is the vertex stabilizer. If $p_c(G)<1$, then $$\\mathbb P_{p_c(G)}(|C_v|=\\infty)=0\\qquad(v\\in G).$$ Consequently, critical percolation has almost surely no infinite cluster.",
          "source_id": "PM-213.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite graph of subexponential growth.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-nilpotent-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph. Suppose that for some $d<\\infty$ there are radii $R_j\\to\\infty$ such that $V_-(R_j)\\le R_j^d$. Then there are a finite-index subgroup $\\Lambda\\le\\operatorname{Aut}(G)$, a finitely generated nilpotent group $N$, and an epimorphism $$\\varrho:\\Lambda\\longrightarrow N$$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\Lambda$ has finitely many vertex orbits;*\n\n2.  *$\\ker\\varrho$ has finite vertex orbits;*\n\n3.  *$\\varrho(\\Lambda_v)$ is finite for every vertex $v$, where $\\Lambda_v=\\{g\\in\\Lambda:gv=v\\}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-213.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-nilpotent-model",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph. Suppose that for some $d<\\infty$ there are radii $R_j\\to\\infty$ such that $V_-(R_j)\\le R_j^d$.           "source_id": "PM-213.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph. Suppose that for some $d<\\infty$ there are radii $R_j\\to\\infty$ such that $V_-(R_j)\\le R_j^d$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank",
        "exact_quote": "Every finitely generated nilpotent group $N$ has a cyclic series. If such a series has $h$ infinite factors, then every subgroup $J\\le N$ is finitely generated and inherits a cyclic series with at most $h$ infinite factors. If $[N:J]=\\infty$, the inherited count is strictly less than $h$. Moreover, if the finitely generated abelianization $N/[N,N]$ has rank at most one, then $[N,N]$ is finite. In this case $N$ is either finite or admits an epimorphism onto $\\mathbb Z$ with finite kernel.",
        "source_id": "PM-213.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Every finitely generated nilpotent group $N$ has a cyclic series. If such a series has $h$ infinite factors, then every subgroup $J\\le N$ is finitely generated and inherits a cyclic series with at most $h$ infinite factors. If $[N:J]=\\infty$, the inherited count is strictly less than $h$. Moreover, if the finitely generated abelianization $N/[N,N]$ has rank at most one, then $[N,N]$ is finite. In this case $N$ is either finite or admits an epimorphism onto $\\mathbb Z$ with finite kernel.",
          "source_id": "PM-213.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Every finitely generated nilpotent group $N$ has a cyclic series. If such a series has $h$ infinite factors, then every subgroup $J\\le N$ is finitely generated and inherits a cyclic series with at most $h$ infinite factors. If $[N:J]=\\infty$, the inherited count is strictly less than $h$. Moreover, if the finitely generated abelianization $N/[N,N]$ has rank at most one, then $[N,N]$ is finite. In this case $N$ is either finite or admits an epimorphism onto $\\mathbb Z$ with finite kernel.",
      "explanation": "Finitely generated nilpotent groups have cyclic series; subgroups inherit them with no more infinite factors, strictly fewer for infinite index, which drives the induction.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank",
          "exact_quote": "If such a series has $h$ infinite factors",
          "source_id": "PM-213.2"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **quasi-transitive graph** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A graph is *quasi-transitive* if its automorphism group has finitely many orbits on vertices.
- **open cluster** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The open cluster $C_x$ of a vertex $x$ consists of the vertices joined to $x$ by finite open paths, including $x$ itself.
- **extremal ball volumes** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Put $V_-(r)=\min_x\|B(x,r)\|$ and $V_+(r)=\max_x\|B(x,r)\|$.
- **associated random object** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A random object is *associated* if any two increasing real-valued functions of it have nonnegative covariance.
- **actual open pivot** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A queried edge is an *actual open pivot* if it is open and closing it destroys $E$ in the full configuration, including unqueried edges.
- **query prefix** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A *query prefix* records the previously queried edges and their states.
- **exact incidence exposure** ([source](#sec:interpolation)): An exact incidence exposure of a cluster records its vertex set and the states of every edge with at least one endpoint in that set, including internal edges skipped by a lazy search.
- **contact** ([source](#pm-h-15)): A contact is a queried edge from the newly explored cluster into $O$.

          "exact_quote": "The open cluster $C_x$ of a vertex $x$ consists of the vertices joined to $x$ by finite open paths, including $x$ itself.",

      "exact_definition": "The open cluster $C_x$ of a vertex $x$ consists of the vertices joined to $x$ by finite open paths, including $x$ itself.",
      "interpretation": "The set of vertices reachable from x along open edges.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "open cluster",
      "scope": "Bernoulli bond percolation on an undirected graph, all sections."
    },
    {

      ],
      "exact_definition": "Put $V_-(r)=\\min_x|B(x,r)|$ and $V_+(r)=\\max_x|B(x,r)|$.",
      "interpretation": "Smallest and largest ball cardinality at radius r; their growth separates the exponential, superpolynomial and polynomial-subsequence cases.",
      "kind": "notation",
      "name": "extremal ball volumes",
      "scope": "Growth analysis for quasi-transitive graphs, Sections on growth alternatives and the two subexponential arguments."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-11",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-11",
          "exact_quote": "A random object is *associated* if any two increasing real-valued functions of it have nonnegative covariance.",
          "source_id": "PM-213.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A random object is *associated* if any two increasing real-valued functions of it have nonnegative covariance.",
      "interpretation": "Positive association for cluster objects ordered by inclusion of vertices and edges.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "associated random object",
      "scope": "Avoidance correlation lemma and its use in the depth-first estimate."
    },

      "interpretation": "An open queried edge whose closure destroys the target connection in the whole configuration, not only in the revealed part.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "actual open pivot",

      "interpretation": "The search's own history, used for conditional probabilities and not any extra cluster information.",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-threshold-lower-bound | critical probability lower bound | ≥ | $1/D$ | critical parameter |
| q-uniform-jump | uniform infinite-cluster probability at criticality | > | $0$ | critical parameter, all vertices |
| q-two-cluster-decay | probability of adjacent large distinct clusters | ≤ | $C s^{-1/2}$ | every edge, every size parameter, parameters at least the fixed lower bound and below one |
| q-dfs-first-moment | first moment of total pivot weight | ≤ | $1$ | parameter strictly between zero and one |
| q-dfs-second-moment | second moment of total pivot weight | ≤ | $3$ | parameter strictly between zero and one |
| q-transcript-entropy-factor | sum of transcript relative entropies | ≤ | $\frac{a^2}{2p(1-p)}D(Q\Vert\mu)$ | overlap at most a on the support of Q |
| q-contact-expectation | expected total contact weight | = | $h_*$ | Bernoulli parameter strictly between zero and one |
| q-interpolation-cost | negative log probability of adjacent large clusters on the chosen path edge | ≤ | $C\bigl(\log(1/\delta)+\lceil\log_2d\rceil+1\bigr)$ | constant depends only on the connection bound and lower bounds for p and q |
| q-separated-pair-constant | separated-pair threshold constant | = | $\theta^2/8$ | fixed subcritical parameter and size scale |
| q-layout-path-bound | path displacement bound | = | $100$ | fixed constant |
| q-layout-slope | corridor axis slope | = | $\tfrac14$ | fixed constant |
| q-layout-entrance-tolerance | initial vertical entrance tolerance | = | $2C+10$ | fixed constant |
| q-layout-tube-halfwidth | tube half-width | = | $3B$ | fixed constant |
| q-prediction-threshold | stored-prediction threshold | = | $\varepsilon/4$ | epsilon chosen to satisfy the contour series condition |
| q-relay-loss | single-relay loss bound | = | $\frac{\varepsilon\delta}{8(L+1)}$ | relay count set by the corridor length |
| q-few-contacts-bound | probability of few shell contacts | < | $\kappa/4$ | for a suitably chosen deterministic level |
| q-transfer-failure | probability of reaching the input region but missing the target | ≤ | $\kappa$ | parameters chosen as in the corridor scales |
| q-gluing-failure-bound | failure probability after reaching a relay | ≤ | $\lambda$ | each relay reaches the target with probability at least one minus lambda |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-orbit-growth** [claim](#stmt-lem-orbit-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-orbit-growth): Fix paths from orbit representatives, translate balls, use subadditivity of the logarithm of the largest ball volume. -> density radius, ball comparisons, existence of the growth rate
- **step-exponential-external** [claim](#stmt-thm-exponential) / [proof](#stmt-thm-exponential): Cite the external theorem for exponential growth. -> no infinite critical cluster in the exponential case
- **step-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-lem-avoidance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-avoidance): Alternating conditional cluster updates, contraction of total variation, preservation of association. -> association and nonpositive cross covariance under avoidance
- **step-two-cluster** [claim](#stmt-prop-two-cluster) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-two-cluster): Marked-cluster discrepancy identity, Doob maximal inequality, then transfer from averages to single edges via orbit density. -> uniform inverse square-root bound for adjacent large distinct clusters
- **step-dfs-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-dfs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dfs): Martingale of conditional connection probabilities and a second tilted martingale charging pairs of pivots. -> first and second moment bounds on total pivot weight
- **step-transcript-entropy** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy): Random priorities with caching, pooling of fresh queries, and an endpoint inequality for polynomials via shifted Legendre expansion. -> sum of transcript entropies bounded by a multiple of the global entropy
- **step-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-prop-interpolation) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Binary subdivision, normalized contact exposures, descriptor conditioning, inverse guessing, entropy bounds. -> a path edge with controlled adjacent large cluster probability
- **step-superpolynomial** [claim](#stmt-thm-superpoly) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-superpoly): Choose a size band, find a nearby separated pair, restrict to a finite ball, apply interpolation against the two-cluster bound. -> no infinite critical cluster in the superpolynomial case
- **step-deleted-residual** [claim](#stmt-lem-deleted-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deleted-residual): Common configuration coupling and monotonicity of clusters under deletion. -> nonnegative increasing residual
- **step-cluster-column** [claim](#stmt-lem-cluster-column) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cluster-column): Downward induction on the index, partition by earliest distinguished vertex, iterate the alternating averaging operator to its stationary limit. -> cone membership and nonnegative inverse coordinates
- **step-joint-gluing** [claim](#stmt-thm-joint-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cluster-column-2): Triangular matrix of conditional cluster probabilities, nonnegative weights from its inverse, then exhaustion of countable graphs. -> joint gluing inequality
- **step-gluing-failure** [claim](#stmt-cor-gluing-failure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-gluing-failure): Subtract the gluing inequality from the relay probability. -> small failure probability after reaching high-probability relays
- **step-cyclic-series** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank): Lower central series, bilinear commutator pairing, intersection of a cyclic series with a subgroup. -> cyclic series, strict decrease of the infinite-factor count for infinite-index subgroups
- **step-nilpotent-model** [claim](#stmt-prop-nilpotent-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nilpotent-model): Auxiliary transitive graph on an orbit, finitary structure theorem for polynomial growth at one scale, lifting to the original graph. -> finite-index subgroup with nilpotent quotient action
- **step-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniqueness): Let the size go to infinity in the two-cluster bound, then force prefix edges open to join distinct infinite clusters. -> uniqueness of the infinite cluster
- **step-bounded-projection** [claim](#stmt-lem-unbounded-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unbounded-projection): Restrict to a component over a bounded box and apply the induction hypothesis with a strictly smaller count. -> no infinite open ray with bounded projection
- **step-approximate-moves** [claim](#stmt-prop-moves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniqueness-2): Direction selection by exit probabilities via winding argument or projected strip, linear normalization, then lowering the parameter. -> two approximate coordinate moves with high probability
- **step-corridors** [claim](#stmt-lem-corridors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-corridors): Compare layer spacings and tube widths in coordinates. -> separation of endpoint regions and corridors
- **step-fresh-collar-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transfer): Choose a shell with enough contacts, define a relay set from independent trials, apply the gluing failure bound conditionally. -> bounded transfer failure across a fresh collar
- **step-subcritical-exploration** [claim](#stmt-prop-exploration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exploration): Adaptive exploration with stored predictions, conditional failure bound per site, contour count of failed sites. -> infinite cluster with positive probability below the critical parameter
- **step-structured** [claim](#stmt-thm-structured) / [proof](#pm-h-31): Induction on the number of infinite cyclic factors; one-dimensional case has threshold one; otherwise contradict via subcritical exploration. -> no infinite critical cluster with a nilpotent quotient action
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exploration-2): Reduce to simple graphs, then split by growth: exponential, superpolynomial, polynomial subsequence. -> critical nonpercolation on quasi-transitive graphs
- **step-lattices** [claim](#stmt-cor-lattices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-lattices): Planar boundary count shows the threshold is below one, then apply the main theorem. -> nonpercolation at criticality on integer lattices

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-critical-nonpercolation**: On an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive undirected graph whose critical probability is below one, Bernoulli bond percolation at the critical probability has almost surely no infinite open cluster, and every vertex lies in an infinite cluster with probability zero.
- **inv-joint-gluing**: For independent edge states on a countable graph, the probability that a vertex connects both to a relay set and to a target set is at least the probability of reaching the relay set times the least relay-to-target connection probability.
- **inv-two-cluster-decay**: On a quasi-transitive graph of subexponential growth, the probability that an edge joins two distinct clusters each of size at least s decays like an inverse square root of s, uniformly over edges.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-networks**: Citing the main theorem to certify absence of large components in a finite network. -> The statement concerns infinite clusters, which cannot occur in a finite graph, so it yields no finite-size information.
- **anti-no-critical-gap**: Applying the conclusion to a graph whose critical probability equals one. -> The hypothesis that the critical probability is below one is required; without it the statement is not asserted.
- **anti-correlated-failures**: Using the result for correlated or cascading edge failures. -> The arguments rely on independent edge states; correlation breaks the product-law estimates.
- **anti-site-percolation**: Reading the lattice corollary as a statement about site percolation or other models. -> The corollary concerns nearest-neighbor Bernoulli bond percolation only.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper's results are probabilistic existence and nonexistence theorems on infinite graphs, proved by contradiction from the assumption that an infinite critical cluster occurs, using limits, tail events and martingale arguments. No finite procedure with explicit steps is given whose output could be executed and checked against an independent oracle.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a nonexistence theorem for infinite graphs. It gives no finite procedure that a program could run on the infinite graph. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Simulating critical percolation on a finite board cannot reproduce an almost-sure statement about infinite clusters. The result gives no finite-size rule for procedural generation or game physics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem does not specify a tool, and it gives no executable certificate for a given graph. The only checkable input is the hypothesis that the critical probability is below one, and the paper verifies that for the lattice case only. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lazy depth-first exploration and the interpolation are proof devices, with conditional probabilities and expectations as inputs. They are not algorithms to be implemented. The main result is existential and almost sure. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is constructed. The corridors and the descriptor counts are analytic bookkeeping inside proofs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no computation to map onto hardware. The entropy and martingale estimates are used only in proofs. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns connectivity of random subgraphs. It gives no hardness assumption, primitive or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper learns, estimates or trains. The relative-entropy bounds control transcripts of query algorithms inside proofs, not a statistical learner. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective is optimised and no relaxation is solved. The joint gluing inequality is a probability lower bound, not a routing or flow guarantee that a solver could use. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The model is independent edge failures on an infinite undirected symmetric graph. Real distributed systems are finite and have correlated failures, so the almost-sure statement has no operational form there. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is pure probability and group theory about critical percolation. Its value is as a theoretical input to further mathematics, with no engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-infinite-graph** (hypothesis): The main theorem is asserted only for an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive undirected graph; on a finite graph every cluster is finite and the statement carries no size information. Blocked use: ['Certifying absence of large components in a finite network from the main theorem', 'Deriving finite-size cluster scaling from the almost-sure statement']
- **limit-critical-gap** (hypothesis): The conclusion needs the critical probability to be below one. At parameter one every edge is open, so an infinite connected graph percolates. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to a graph whose critical probability equals one']
- **limit-quasi-transitivity** (hypothesis): Quasi-transitivity, meaning finitely many vertex orbits, supplies the uniform constants used throughout: a bounded degree, orbit density and a uniform positive lower bound at criticality under the contradiction hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Using the result for scale-free or inhomogeneous graphs without finitely many orbits', 'Quoting the uniform lower bound for graphs lacking orbit density']
- **limit-independent-bonds** (hypothesis): The estimates use independent edge states: Harris correlation, product laws outside exposed clusters and conditional gluing. The model is bond percolation, not site percolation. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to correlated or cascading failures', 'Reading the main result or the lattice corollary as a site percolation statement for general graphs']
- **limit-critical-parameter-only** (edge_case): The assertion is made exactly at the critical parameter; it says nothing about supercritical parameters, where an infinite cluster occurs by the definition of the threshold. Blocked use: ['Arguing that clusters are bounded in a supercritical regime']
- **limit-exponential-external** (nonconstructive): The exponential growth case is imported from an external theorem and is not proved here; the subexponential cases are proved by contradiction with ineffective constants. Blocked use: ['Extracting explicit constants or an algorithm from the proof', "Treating the exponential case as established by the paper's own arguments"]
- **limit-two-cluster-regime** (formal_scope): The inverse square-root bound for adjacent large distinct clusters needs subexponential growth and a fixed positive lower bound on the parameter; its constant depends on the graph and that bound. Blocked use: ['Using the bound on exponential growth graphs', 'Using a constant uniform over all graphs or down to parameter zero']
- **limit-entropy-overlap** (hypothesis): The transcript entropy bound requires deterministic terminating algorithms, each bit read at most once per algorithm, and an overlap bound a that holds on the support of Q. Blocked use: ['Applying it to randomized or non-terminating query procedures', 'Applying it when no overlap bound holds on the support of Q']
- **limit-interpolation-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Interpolation needs a simple path with uniform pairwise connection lower bound for pairs of indices strictly closer than the path length, and a positive probability that the endpoint clusters are distinct and large. Blocked use: ['Applying it without the connection lower bound for intermediate pairs', 'Applying it when the endpoint event has probability zero']
- **limit-gluing-countable** (hypothesis): Joint gluing is proved for countably many vertices and edges with independent edge probabilities and a nonempty relay set; the finite-graph proof first assumes every edge probability is below one and extends by approximation and exhaustion. Blocked use: ['Using it with dependent edges', 'Using it with an empty relay set as an inequality for the joint event']
- **limit-structured-action** (hypothesis): The nilpotent quotient theorem requires subexponential growth, finitely many vertex orbits, a kernel with finite vertex orbits and finite images of vertex stabilizers; the one-dimensional quotient case has threshold one. Blocked use: ['Applying it to actions whose quotient has infinite stabilizer images', 'Using it when the nilpotent quotient maps onto the integers with finite kernel']
- **limit-exploration-parameters** (approximation): The subcritical exploration works only for the chosen scale hierarchy: the failure threshold, loss and contour series conditions must hold together, and each relay needs a fresh collar with wires outside it. Blocked use: ['Reusing the construction with arbitrary parameters violating the stated inequalities', 'Revealing an outgoing corridor before the incoming one is tested']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 213 (`lean/docs/213.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Critical bond and site percolation on the cubic lattice](../../preprints/Critical-bond-and-site-percolation-on-the-cubic-lattice-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [No percolation at criticality on quasi-transitive graphs](../../preprints/No-percolation-at-criticality-on-quasi-transitive-graphs-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The critical-percolation question asks whether an infinite cluster can remain at the threshold. The formalized result proves that, at their respective critical probabilities, nearest-neighbor bond and site percolation on $\mathbb Z^3$ almost surely have no infinite cluster. Equivalently, almost surely every vertex belongs to a finite cluster in each model.
>
> The formalization proves absence of an infinite cluster at criticality for Bernoulli bond percolation on every infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with critical probability $p_c<1$. At $p=p_c$, the probability that any infinite cluster exists is zero. The graph model permits bond multiplicities.

Comparator source `CriticalPercolation.lean` (2253 bytes, sha256 `cff7e946579873434b64ed4679d21ad9f07ac77958af69952d28826136b7e528`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open Set MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory

namespace CriticalPercolation

/-- An undirected graph with labelled bonds, including loops and parallel bonds. -/
structure BondGraph (V E : Type*) where
  ends : E → Sym2 V

namespace BondGraph
variable {V E : Type*} (G : BondGraph V E)

/-- The graph of retained bonds. `fromEdgeSet` discards loops. -/
def openGraph (ω : Set E) : SimpleGraph V :=
  SimpleGraph.fromEdgeSet (G.ends '' ω)

/-- The underlying connectivity graph with every bond retained. -/
def fullGraph : SimpleGraph V := G.openGraph Set.univ

/-- Local finiteness counts bonds, not just distinct neighbors. -/
def LocallyFinite : Prop := ∀ v : V, {e : E | v ∈ G.ends e}.Finite

/-- Automorphisms preserve the vertex and bond structure, including multiplicities. -/
structure Aut where
  vertex : V ≃ V
  edge : E ≃ E
  preserves : ∀ e, G.ends (edge e) = Sym2.map vertex (G.ends e)

/-- Finitely many vertex orbits under the full automorphism group. -/
def QuasiTransitive : Prop :=
  ∃ S : Set V, S.Finite ∧ ∀ v : V, ∃ s ∈ S, ∃ φ : G.Aut, φ.vertex s = v

/-- Finite open paths, including the path of length zero, define the open cluster. -/
def cluster (ω : Set E) (v : V) : Set V := {w | (G.openGraph ω).Reachable v w}

/-- The event that at least one open cluster is infinite. -/
def percolates : Set (Set E) := {ω | ∃ v : V, (G.cluster ω v).Infinite}

/-- Independent retention with parameter `p ∈ [0,1]` of each labelled bond. -/
noncomputable def law (_G : BondGraph V E) (p : unitInterval) : Measure (Set E) :=
  ProbabilityTheory.setBernoulli Set.univ p

/-- The infimum of parameters with positive probability of an infinite cluster. -/
noncomputable def criticalProbability : unitInterval :=
  sInf {p : unitInterval | 0 < G.law p G.percolates}

/-- Critical Bernoulli bond percolation has no infinite cluster when the critical
probability is below one. -/
theorem no_percolation_at_criticality [Infinite V]
    (hconnected : G.fullGraph.Connected)
    (hlocal : G.LocallyFinite)
    (hquasi : G.QuasiTransitive)
    (hpc : G.criticalProbability < 1) :
    G.law G.criticalProbability G.percolates = 0 := by
  sorry

end BondGraph
end CriticalPercolation

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that critical Bernoulli bond percolation has no infinite cluster on any infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with critical probability less than one. This resolves the bond form of the criticality conjecture of Benjamini and Schramm.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive undirected graph. |
| [stmt-lem-orbit-growth](#stmt-lem-orbit-growth) | lemma | `lem:orbit-growth` | 2 | There is an integer $C_0$ such that every vertex orbit meets every ball of radius $C_0$. |
| [stmt-thm-exponential](#stmt-thm-exponential) | theorem | `thm:exponential` | 3 | ). *If a connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph satisfies $\liminf_{r\to\infty}&#124;B(x,r)&#124;^{1/r}>1$, then it has almost surely no infinite Bernoulli bond cluster at its critical probability.* |
| [stmt-prop-two-cluster](#stmt-prop-two-cluster) | proposition | `prop:two-cluster` | 4 | Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph of subexponential growth. |
| [stmt-lem-avoidance](#stmt-lem-avoidance) | lemma | `lem:avoidance` | 5 | Let $G$ be a finite undirected graph with independent edge probabilities strictly between zero and one. |
| [stmt-lem-dfs](#stmt-lem-dfs) | lemma | `lem:dfs` | 6 | For the preceding search, let $$W=\sum_{e\text{ queried actual open pivot}}q d_e.$$ Then $\mathbb E_\mu W\le1$ and $\mathbb E_\mu W^2\le3$.* |
| [stmt-lem-entropy](#stmt-lem-entropy) | lemma | `lem:entropy` | 7 | Let $\mu$ be the Bernoulli product law of parameter $0<p<1$ on a finite set of bits, and let $Q$ be any probability law on those bits. |
| [stmt-prop-interpolation](#stmt-prop-interpolation) | proposition | `prop:interpolation` | 8 | Let $\mu$ be Bernoulli bond percolation with parameter $p\in(0,1)$ on a finite undirected graph, and put $q=1-p$. |
| [stmt-thm-superpoly](#stmt-thm-superpoly) | theorem | `thm:superpoly` | 9 | Let $G$ be an infinite connected locally finite quasi-transitive graph with $p_c<1$. |
